## Document 305: AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI boom or doom_ what will superintelligence feel like and when will we get there_ (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

AI boom or doom: what will 
superintelligence feel like and  
when will we get there?

1 
Authors 
Adrian Cox 
Research Analyst

Azeem Azhar, Exponential View 
Expert Collaborator

October 12, 2026 
The recent panic about rogue AI agents collaborating in swarms to take over the 
internet and threaten human existence has, more than occasionally, lacked 
nuance. But it does reflect how artificial intelligence is progressing in creative 
and surprising ways, and at a pace that seems uncontrollable. 
For some in the industry, it is evidence that the technology is following a path of 
accelerating self-improvement that will lead to artificial general intelligence (AGI) 
and, perhaps, to utopia or catastrophe. 
Former Google CEO Eric Schmidt has sketched out a “San Francisco 
Consensus” based on a belief in scaling laws, with ever larger AI models 
becoming an input into their own improvement; superintelligence by the end of 
the decade; and transformative AI systems outperforming humans on many 
tasks. This loop would lead to exponential growth in scientific advancement, 
financial returns and human progress. And crucially, AGI, and then 
superintelligence, would merely be milestones in a process, far from its climax. 
If that were the case, superintelligence could make much of the cognitive work 
that dominates the world economy much cheaper and change the ways in which 
firms compete. But its economic effects will depend on how businesses and 
customers respond. 
In this paper, we analyse what the San Francisco Consensus means for investors 
and enterprises in the years to come and what could accelerate – or slow down – 
its timeline of AGI by 2030. 
1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
3. Can we get there practically? 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
The question is urgent. 
And it is complicated by reports of AI models autonomously accessing the 
internet and hacking other companies; a public backlash over the energy-
intensive data centres springing up as part of a historic $1 trillion a year 
investment boom; and dire warnings of threats to life if superintelligent AI runs 
roughshod over its human creators to achieve its own goals.

### Page 2

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

1. What would superintelligence look and feel like? 
1.1 What are AGI and superintelligence? 
Artificial general intelligence (AGI) is a simple milestone scribbled in pencil: an AI 
system that has surpassed human capabilities. Superintelligence is the 
continuum beyond that point. It is not merely systems that exceed the best 
human performance across one cognitive task or another, but system s that 
exceed human potential across every cognitive task. 
The San Francisco Consensus takes this further: the intelligence must be able to 
produce more intelligence. The key to getting there is so -called recursive self -
improvement, where the AI is able to autonomously design and improve itself in 
a way that compounds exponentially. A first step has maybe already been taken, 
with OpenAI saying recently that AI is now writing approximately 80 percent of 
the company’s code. 
There is significant disagreement on how we will be able to identify AGI or 
superintelligence when we see it, let alone what comes afterwards.  
OpenAI’s Charter defines AGI in economic terms, as “highly autonomous systems 
that outperform humans at the most economically valuable work”. Google 
researchers proposed a five -step ladder based on performance and generality, 
with level 5 “superhuman” for a system that outperforms 100% of humans. And 
philosopher Nick Bostrom, who got the ball rolling in 2014 with his influential 
book “Superintelligenc e”, defined it as “any intellect that greatly exceeds the 
cognitive performance of humans in virtually all domains of interest”. 
It’s also a moving target.  
In August, OpenAI CEO Sam Altman said the company would have an internal 
system that he would call AGI by later this year but subsequently told a podcast 
that “at best it’s a very poorly defined term. I was going to say it’s like an irrelevant 
marketing term”. Yet his No. 2 Greg Brockman described the new Astra model at 
the start of September as being potentially the moment that “AGI was created”. 
For this report, we have chosen to focus on a system that outperforms essentially 
all humans across essentially all cognitive tasks, including the development of AI 
itself.  
Under our scenario, by 2030 the best performing AI models would have the 
qualities of “superintelligence”, that is better than human experts in all domains. 
These would be frontier models: and using them would feel exactly like bringing 
the best human experts in a room to solve whatever business problem you faced.

And the equivalents of today’s frontier models would be available, quite literally, 
everywhere, compressed enough to run on every phone, tablet or laptop 
computer.  
In early 2026, we already see early examples of what highly capable AI can do. 
They give a flavour for a world with superintelligence even if the reality would be 
a leap beyond, both quantitatively and qualitatively. At that point, 
superintelligence would mark a regime break, not a faster version of what we 
already have. Rather than blindly doing what humans specify, but just a bit better, 
it would specify the task itself.

### Page 3

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 3

Figure 1: What changes when cognitive work gets abundantly cheaper?

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Meanwhile cost may be less and less of a constraint as ever greater machine 
intelligence becomes cheaply available. Of measured technologies, no 
technology has got cheaper faster than AI has, almost halving in price every 
quarter for the past three years on a like-for-like basis, or 13 times per year, Epoch 
data show. 
If these systems can be supplied widely, firms will be able to buy more of the 
complex, expensive and rare analysis, design and engineering kinds of services 
than they do today. Activities that were previously uneconomic could become 
viable, and in a competitive market, savings could pass to customers. The main 
driver will then be whether demand grows fast enough to preserve margins. 
However, superior capability, doesn’t, on its own, automatically lead to the 
adoption of cheap, abundant intelligence. Tasks that do not yet yield to super-

### Page 4

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

intelligence, perhaps because they require real world-coordination, will therefore 
end up commanding significant premia. 
At that point, cognitive labour stops being scarce and the bottleneck becomes 
the physical and practical assets that cannot be conjured by up more compute, 
such as electricity, land, steel and permits. 
1.2 Capability and diffusion 
If AI becomes capable of improving itself, the technology may compound faster 
and faster. The supply of machine intelligence could grow faster than business 
and physical infrastructure can accommodate.  
This balance between capability and diffusion creates different scenarios.

Figure 2: The balance between capabilities and diffusion

Source: Deutsche Bank Research

Lower capability, constrained diffusion: where we are now 
Progress is steady and sector-by-sector. This is how technology usually diffuses, 
according to researchers for the New York Fed back in 2003, who tracked 20 
major technologies across leading industrial economies from 1788 and found “a 
pattern of trickle-down diffusion that is remarkably robust across technologies”. 
Lower capability, high diffusion: the cloud transition 
This is faster because the complementary investment is small, with no retooling 
and no skills at scale required. Amazon Web Services launched the modern cloud 
in 2006, offering scalable computing and storage infrastructure. Adoption grew 
and then accelerated over the pandemic. Almost all enterprises now use it  in 
some form.

### Page 5

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

Higher capability, lower diffusion: the overhang 
This is theoretically possible and arguably where we are today in narrow domains. 
The technology would be applied in some sectors of the economy, while other 
sectors would move at a slower speed.  Superintelligent or not, it risks being 
bogged down by physical constraints, bureaucracy and human inertia. 
Higher capability, higher diffusion: superintelligence 
In this scenario, capability becomes so high that it changes the dynamics of 
diffusion itself. AI generates not just better AI (so -called recursive self -
improvement, or RSI) but also speeds up the integration process. 
Rapid AI diffusion could work two ways. 
In the first, AI could be enlisted to do integration work itself: mapping processes, 
rebuilding data work, retraining staff, writing the control and audit layer, and so 
on. The problem with this is that absorbing and recreating undocumented 
knowledge is impossible by definition, at least for a task-based superintelligence.  
The second way that higher capability AI could be rapidly diffused is much more 
competitive – and disruptive. Rather than incumbents slowly converting AI, 
perhaps using it for some of the hard work of integration, they could be instead 
displaced by new firms that have been built AI-native from day one. New entrants 
will never have to incur integration costs because there is nothing to integrate. 
Incumbents will need to change – fast – or die.  
Change could also be forced by earlier adopters in the supply chain, as happened 
with one company after another adopting electronic data interchange (EDI) to 
link with its suppliers or customers, or by a migration of capital towards early 
adopters, reprici ng the cost of capital for slow -coaches and prodding them to 
move faster. 
1.3 The forcing mechanism: change or die 
A company that uses superintelligence may outperform one that doesn’t. 
Firms using superintelligence will likely have substantial cost and revenue -
harvesting advantages. Every firm will therefore have to adopt superintelligence 
or retrench to a smaller set of offerings. And any business line of lasting value 
abandoned by a no n-adopter will be taken over by an adopter. Customer inertia 
will provide a safe harbour for a limited time only.  
The analogy here is electricity. There came a point in the deployment of 
electricity where firms in every sector, from entertainment to finance, from 
healthcare to transport, had to adopt the technology to remain relevant.  
Under this scenario, wait -and-see will not be an option. There will be no stable 
“non-adoption” alternative. Sufficiently high capabilities will force a tipping point 
of rapid diffusion and competitive repricing. Firms using yesterday’s production 
model will either have to change, choose a different market or die.  
Of course, caveats apply.  
Customers may not change behaviour immediately. They may even prefer 
companies which limit their use of AI, in the same way that some customers 
choose to avoid factory-produced food.

### Page 6

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

The paradox that Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Solow observed in 1987, 
that “you can see the computer age everywhere but in the productivity statistics”, 
is likely to still apply for a while – even with superintelligence. 
The reason is that the productivity J -curve identified by Stanford University AI 
economy guru Erik Brynjolfsson et al is unavoidable. In that scenario, the drag of 
early intangible investments to integrate general purpose technologies means 
that productivity initially appears to slow before rapidly accelerating.

Figure 3: Productivity J-curve: slow then fast

Source: Brynjolfsson E, Rock D, Syverson C, “The Productivity J-Curve” (2018, revised 2020), Deutsche Bank Research

Furthermore, “change or die” would require abundant capital and low regulatory 
entry points for nimble new entrants. But in practice, highly regulated areas such 
as banking, utilities, pharmaceuticals and defence are tough nuts to crack. 
Existing contracts and licences, high switching costs and decades of depreciated 
capital investment and infrastructure would shield incumbents at least for a 
healthy transition period. 
For an extreme example from the real world of better, cheaper technology failing 
to force change, take Pakistan’s 3.6 -gigawatt imported coal power plant fleet 
installed in the mid -2010s. In that case, it came with expensive take -or-pay 
liabilities which wi ll continue to run for decades, even though changes like 
cheaper rooftop solar have cut utilisation to below 20 percent.  
So: superintelligence will not force adoption everywhere. It will hit hardest in 
contestable sectors, say, high -volume customer services, routine software 
engineering and freelance creative work, and widen the gap between them and 
protected ones. Forced adoption will be about relative value, not an apocalypse.

### Page 7

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

1.4 What survives and what it means for portfolios 
Because AI sits on an aggressive learning curve, its price on an equivalent quality 
basis is plummeting. In a world of recursive super intelligence this is likely to 
continue. Its price will fall to the cost of compute.  
What will retain its value will be whatever the model cannot replicate: 
1. Proprietary data 
2. The right to operate , particularly in heavily regulated sectors such as 
healthcare and finance. 
3. Sticky customer relationships,  such as those where mission -critical 
workflows and databases are deeply entwined. 
4. Excludable physical assets, such as machinery and land. 
5. In addition, humans have proven to have unlimited wants.  Each shift in 
technological capabilities or cultural norms has delivered new needs to 
be met. Some will be needs we can articulate today, such as originality or 
empathy, but new ones are likely to arise.  
If this forcing mechanism holds, firms will separate into two “bimodal” groups: 
adopters and casualties, with very little in between. 
This dispersion of performance may create challenges for investors. The four 
years since the launch of ChatGPT in November 2022 have seen the stocks of 
semiconductor and semiconductor equipment makers surge almost seven times 
while those of software makers  have only risen by 80 percent. Conventional  
diversification and volatility-based allocation may become ineffective strategies.

Figure 4: Total return for S&P 500 semiconductor vs software stocks since 
launch of ChatGPT on Nov 30, 2022

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 8

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 8

Diversification normally works because idiosyncratic risks are unrelated and 
average each other out. Here they would not: with one variable that overrides 
everything else, spreading capital across more names would just spread exposure 
rather than diluting it. 
Investors will need to consider adapting their approach in three ways: 
1. Classify investments as adopters or casualties rather than position off 
averages.  
• Under a normal distribution, errors are symmetric and partially 
cancel each other out. But under bimodality they do not: to put a firm 
in one category or another means getting it completely right or 
wrong. Position size should track confidence in the classification. 
2. Avoid reading calm price action as low risk.  
• Trailing volatility measures dispersion that has already been realised. 
Bimodal separation is a jump, where the two modes look identical 
right until the moment when they don’t. A classic example is Chegg, 
an educational software maker which traded like a normal small-cap 
stock until one day in May 2023 when it halved on a single day after 
management linked slowing growth in new customers to ChatGPT. 
3. Averaging down, ie  scooping up shares whose price has fallen, may no 
longer be prudent. 
2. Can we get there theoretically? 
2.1 Scaling laws 
Scaling laws have provided the blueprint for improving AI over the past decade. 
It was a relationship between the amount of data, the amount of compute and 
the complexity of an AI model somewhat predictably informed how powerful a 
model would become. Google DeepMind’s Chinchilla model in 2022 showed that 
you could compensate for having a smaller model by using substantially more 
data. 
Scaling is older than the chatbot. In 1950 Claude Shannon built Theseus, a 
mechanical mouse that learned its way through a maze by trial and error. Epoch 
AI reckons it took about 40 operations of compute, known as FLOPs, to train. Grok 
4, trained in 2025, took roughly ten trillion trillion times as much. Across those 25 
orders of magnitude, more compute has brought more capability.

• A falling price may be information that a business model is obsolete 
rather than an opportunity.

### Page 9

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

Figure 5: Computation used to train notable AI systems (petaFLOP): log scale

Source: Epoch AI (citing estimates from AI literature), Our World in Data, Deutsche Bank Research

It is the robustness of this relationship that has made larger investments in 
compute capex a sine qua non for AI companies. 
However, this is not a law of physics and nothing guarantees it will continue . 
Indeed, in the ChatGPT era, five different approaches to scaling have been 
applied. Older strategies yield worse results, and new techniques, such as post -
training refinements, have been developed. The consensus amongst American 
and Chinese AI labs is that these will hold.  
While this may be a consensus amongst the commercial labs, there is scientific 
disagreement that apply ing scaling laws to today’s large -language models can 
achieve superintelligence. 
Yann LeCun, a Turing Award winner and formerly Meta's chief AI scientist, is blunt: 
"LLMs are not a path to superintelligence or even human-level intelligence”. Even 
inside the labs, the recipe is contested. Demis Hassabis of Google DeepMind has 
said he expects "one or two more big breakthroughs" to be needed "in addition to 
scaling". Ilya Sutskever, a co -founder and former chief scientist of OpenAI, has 
said the field is going "back to the age of research again, just with big computers". 
This is a scientific argument we are not in a position to evaluate , but for the 
purposes of our scenario, we will side with the labs’ position. 
For the moment, there is strong evidence that capabilities have in fact 
accelerated over the past three years, as reasoning models have outpaced non -
reasoning models by two or three times, according to Epoch data.

### Page 10

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 10

2.2 Recursive self-improvement: AI improving AI 
A cocktail-party definition of recursive self-improvement (RSI) is AI that improves 
itself. The reality is that it can be used in two ways: 
1. Self-improving agents that get better at using themselves: showing 
smarter use of prompts, tools, memory and workflows. The model -
building process is untouched. 
2. True RSI improves the process that builds the models: designing 
experiments, curating data, optimising algorithms and designing chips in 
a compounding loop. 
The former is like a factory worker who gets better at the job by learning the quirks 
of the machinery, getting organised and making fewer mistakes . The latter is like 
the worker redesigning the machine tools and then using those to build the next 
generation of machines. 
True RSI is what OpenAI’s chief scientist Jacob Pachoki referred to last month in 
a blog post about  “An Alien Mind ”. “Automated AI research is a more dramatic 
form of scaling intelligence with compute,” he wrote. “And of course as a part of 
it, AI will improve the computational substrate itself,” he added. Pachoki 
represents a broadly held consensus view amongst America n and Chinese labs 
that RSI is both possible and necessary for AI development. 
• OpenAI (June 2026): “AI doing AI research will become the determining 
factor of the pace of progress within the next few years.” 
• Google DeepMind’s Demis Hassabis (May 2026): “All the leading labs are 
quite focused on [RSI]…” Humanity is standing in the “foothills of the 
singularity”. 
• Alibaba CEO Eddie Wu (Sept 2026): RSI is “a concrete path forward,” with 
models autonomously designing experiments and driving a cycle of self-
evolution. 
For now, self -improving agents are the AI du jour. Noisy claims about the vast 
majority of coding being done by agents do not always stand up in the light of day 
from companies still hiring human coders at breakneck pace, albeit no longer to 
do the most mundane parts of the job.  
A better metric may be METR’s task -completion time horizon. This is measure of 
the complexity of software tasks that an AI agent can complete on its own, using 
the baseline of how long it would take a human expert to finish the task, at a 50 
percent success rate. So far the most advanced models have reached about 16 
hours, up from OpenAI’s GPT-4’s four minutes in March 2023.

### Page 11

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

Figure 6: Task horizon of software tasks that leading frontier models can complete 50% of the time (log scale)

Source: METR, Deutsche Bank Research

But software engineers can hold off on updating their resumes a little longer: 
there is a long road between 50 percent accuracy and the 99 percent or 100 
percent needed for serious enterprise use at scale, with the horizon potentially 
getting further away the nearer you get. Also METR notes that measurements 
above 16 hours are unreliable with the current task suite. As is the case across AI, 
the instrument is running out before the question is settled.  
More broadly, there are significant limitations to RSI:  
1. Research is clock-bound.  
• A frontier training run can take weeks on thousands of specialised chips, 
and researchers often cannot design the next experiment until the 
previous one has been completed.  
• That means that the speed of the loop is set by experiments completed 
per hour or day, not by ideas generated.  
• Compute is a hard ceiling that needs to be bought, buil t and connected 
to a grid. 
2. The real world defies verifiability.  
• The loop compounds only when the system can check its own output 
cheaply and automatically. 
• It is no coincidence that AI loves maths and coding. It is much harder to 
verify outputs over a long time horizon in the real world: does this drug 
work or does this strategy succeed?  
• RSI compounds fastest where the economy cares least and slows where 
the returns are largest.

### Page 12

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

3. Sequences are consequential.  
• Amdahl’s law finds that speed -up is capped when processes run in a 
sequence.  
• If you automate 90 percent, the residual 10 percent still limits you to a 
ten-times faster process. Nine women cannot produce a baby in one 
month.  
• The Usain Bolt of AI still has to run alongside the comparative tortoises 
of the training run itself, chip fabrication and real -world validation. Ten 
times faster is still not a regime break. 
This is borne out by METR’s series. Doubling every seven months is fast 
exponential growth but a take -off would show the doubling time itself – the 
second derivative – shrinking. Horizons are not (yet) exploding. 
Philosopher Toby Ord shows that recursive self -improvement can only reach a 
singularity if each cycle of improvement takes less time than the last, shrinking 
fast towards zero. He argues that real feedback loops have a floor: chips take time 
to make and models take time to train. A I progress could still outrun an 
exponential, but it would not go vertical. 
2.3 Scaling plus RSI: a match made in the race to AGI? 
No scaling and RSI means no short timeline for AGI. Without scaling, more 
compute buys nothing and capital is wasted. Without RSI, AI capability will 
continue to improve at the pace of flesh -and-blood human researchers, 
augmented by AI tools but not replac ed. Only with both will it be possible to 
produce a system that improves faster than the humans building it. Without one 
or the other, no quantity of chips, energy or capital will fix it. 
3. Can we get there practically? 
3.1 Infrastructure, chips and energy 
There is no consensus on the amount of training required to reach AGI. Estimates 
suggest that training 2026's top models required between 10^26 and 10^27 
FLOPs. If we hypothesise that an "AGI -like model" would require three orders of 
magnitude more training, this would require 10^30 FLOPs.   
Consider today’s US AI infrastructure, which runs to approximately 10^22 FLOPs. 
If all of this was conceivably used for a 10^30 FLOP training run, it would take 
nearly four years to complete.  
The physical infrastructure to practically support such a training run would 
require enormous power, perhaps around 6GW, about 50% more than the four 
Vogtle nuclear power plants recently activated in the US. The cost of the 
compute infrastructure would run to between $300bn and $500bn.  
Today’s advanced chips would simply not suffice. Back-of-the-envelope maths 
suggests that even using Nvidia’s most advanced Vera Rubin chips, training such 
a model in four months would require 7.8m chips and 26GW of facility power. 
That would cost $900bn today and a rental equivalent cost of $90bn.  
Of course, frontier compute is growing rapidly, and price -performance is 
declining. If current trends hold, a 2030 -era cluster capable of such a run would

### Page 13

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 13

need perhaps $200bn in computer and power hardware for a training run costing 
about $20bn.

Figure 7: Technology cost index over time (years since start of price decline)

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

The hyperscalers are leading the charge towards AGI. Alphabet, Amazon, Meta, 
Microsoft and Oracle are set to invest almost $800bn in capital expenditure this 
year, mostly for ever more powerful data centres, and over $1 trillion a year 
through 2030 and beyond.

Figure 8: Hyperscaler capital expenditure (including estimates)

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research

### Page 14

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 14

By 2030, assuming no market retrenchment, this means additional $4 trillion to 
$6 trillion on AI compute capability invested. This infrastructure could 
accommodate a training run of 10^30 FLOPs with some comfort.  
But even if AI labs crack the secret sauce of RSI, and their finance chiefs are willing 
to invest in such large training runs, and hyperscalers are prepared to continue to 
build the infrastructure, the infrastructure needs to be built.  
Grid connection queues, turbine lead times and transmission build are measured 
in years, not the months it takes to assemble and air-freight the latest Nvidia chip. 
The lead time for substation transformers is currently as long as three years. 
Politics in the US has shifted towards slowing the pace of the AI build out and this 
force will run against the demands for larger data centres that would be essential 
for RSI.  
3.2 Capital 
Financing for AI has been abundant in the historic buildout of the past three years. 
However, it is still largely conditional not on revenue but on belief in a future 
payoff. 
RSI is a “key part of the investment thesis” in the “biggest scientific bet civilisation 
has ever made,” Google DeepMind’s chief strategy officer Jasjeet Sekhon said at 
a conference in August, The Information reported. “We have this danger we could 
hit an AI air pocket such that the expenditures happen but the revenues don’t 
show up.” 
An inflection point is approaching where the nature of investment changes from 
free cash flow and equity to debt, with all that entails in terms of exposure to 
rising rates and creditor appetite. The hyperscalers are reaching a point where 
capex is no long er covered by operating cashflow and where net debt exceeds 
earnings before interest, depreciation and amortisation (EBITDA). 
Alphabet, for example, posted its first quarterly free  cash outflow in nearly two 
decades in its most recent quarter, even as it lifted its full-year capex guidance to 
$205bn. It said it would continue to rent third -party capacity and reported a 
backlog of $514bn in contracted, not-yet-recognised data centre revenue. 
3.3 Data 
Data has been the new oil for some time now. But unfortunately for AI 
development it seems to be running out even faster. 
The effective stock of quality - and repetition -adjusted public human text was 
about 300 trillion tokens, with a vast 90 percent confidence interval of 100 trillion 
to 1,000 trillion, Epoch estimated in 2024. A token is roughly equivalent to three 
quarters of a word. By that count it was likely to be fully used between 2026 and 
2032.

### Page 15

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 15

Figure 9: Projections of stock of public text and data usage

Source: Epoch AI, Deutsche Bank Research

However, since then, rather than data dropping off a cliff, there has been a rise in 
synthetic data generation, transfer learning from data-rich domains such as video 
and telemetry, and data efficiency. While the data shortage has not been 
resolved, the immediate crisis has been averted. 
AI now makes much of its own training data. When DeepMind's AlphaGo became 
superhuman at playing the mind-bendingly complex board game Go, it did so not 
by playing human experts, but by playing millions and millions of games against 
itself. 
For many domains relating to coding and mathematical proofs where automatic 
checks are possible, such synthetic experience can move things forward.  That is 
the case with AI research, where the data may be the results of experiments, all 
of which are bought by compute. About 90% of OpenAI's 2024 research compute 
went on such experiments. 
And increasingly, people write the tests rather than the answers. By the summer 
of 2026, selling training data for practi ce tasks was a nearly $9bn revenue 
business. Firms such as Mercor hire experts to generate long tasks against which 
the AI can be trained. 
The challenge here is that trust in the checker becomes a scarce input.

### Page 16

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 16

As AI makes production cheaper, it also makes establishing that production is 
accurate or reliable more expensive.  Domains which can be checked 
mechanically, such as mathematics, will be opened up more quickly. But those 
which rely on expert verification, such as medicine, engineering or law, may 
progress more slowly. 
The likely implications are twofold: a slower rate of diffusion into economic 
domains that are less verifiable and a larger share of production costs devoted to 
assurance. 
Another issue is that s ystems that have to  grade themselves learn to game the 
process. T his was partially what happened when an OpenAI model escap ed a 
sandbox and hacked into a third-party website. Trust replaced data as a wall. 
There are also question marks over the quality of data that is recycled repeatedly 
through the system. Although humans continue to produce original content, half 
of internet articles are now primarily generated by AI, Graphite data show.

Figure 10: Human vs primarily AI-generated articles on the internet

Source: Graphite (citing 55.4k articles from Common Crawl examined by AI detectors), Deutsche Bank Research

3.4 An incomplete case 
This analysis shows that the San Francisco Consensus, which emphasises RSI and 
a short timeline to AGI, is neither predestined nor impossible.  
Rather it depends on whether the science is right, on whether scaling is all you 
need, and on whether the infrastructure and capital required for that scaling is 
deployed and accessible to the leading labs.  
There is, in other words, a possible rather than impossible, path to AGI, subject to 
those conditions.  
None of this proves that superintelligence will ever arrive. A buildup at this scale 
has few peacetime precedents and vastly exceeds the likes of the Manhattan 
Project and Apollo programme.

### Page 17

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 17

It is uncertain whether there will be enough demand to pay upfront for the 
investment in achieving RSI that would justify that investment. And whether the 
friction of verification, and inevitable declining marginal returns, act as a drag on 
what otherwise might be a runaway process. 
4. What happens if we don’t get there? The base case 
4.1 Separating AI progress from superintelligence 
So what if that thesis is wrong? Does the AI trade depend on a journey to AGI?  
AI can remain economically important even before RSI coupled with scaling laws 
lead to super intelligence . Companies do not need to wait for nebulous 
superintelligence to generate identifiable returns from AI in the medium term as 
they fold it into their systems, streamline workflows and roll out new business 
models. 
Investors already have exposure to RSI as a capital allocation because the leading 
US labs say RSI is a key part of their product strategy. And it is these labs whose 
products are catalysing and amplifying the infrastructure boom. 
Even r apid competitive diffusion does not mean every part of the physical 
economy changes instantly. 
The smartest superintelligence might identify the optimal buyout structure 
before breakfast, but it cannot force a financially constrained buyer to use it or 
persuade the seller to accept less than they have been expecting. Those 
challenges will be even more intractable where the parties are more complex, say, 
fiscally constrained sovereigns or foreign equity holders. 
4.2 Why the base case is attractive 
Superintelligence would be a chaotic accelerant to human society, and a 
potential regime break. But it is not the only scenario in which today’s investment 
in AI infrastructure makes economic sense. 
Adoption of AI tools by businesses is progressing approximately three times faster 
than the mobile, internet and cloud computing waves. From sector revenues 
below $1bn in 2022, the AI economy, excluding China, will likely exceed $200bn 
in revenues in 2026.  
Early winners are the cloud businesses. Google Cloud revenue jumped 82 percent 
from a year earlier in the most recent quarter as companies turned AI pilots into 
workflows. Amazon Web Services sales were up 37 percent and Microsoft’s AI 
business more than doubled. 
There is also slow, but increasing, evidence of a productivity uplift. The J-curve 
cuts both ways: front -loading investments is taking one step back to take two 
steps forward. The so -called complementary intangible investment that is 
(mis)measured as cost today to the detriment of the productivity statistics will 
flatter them in the years to come as it pays off. The base case does not require a 
regime break to show up as a productivity surprise, just for the accounting to 
catch up. 
The economic gains from AI that is less powerful than the AGI mooted by the labs 
would be substantial. And in this base case, they may also be attractive.

### Page 18

12 October 2026 
Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 18

Hyperscaler capex is driving earnings at beneficiaries ranging from 
semiconductor makers to construction companies and accounting for between 
one-third and one -half of current US growth. The wealth effect of rising stock 
prices, with  the ratio of  US equity wealth to income rising from around 1.8 to 
almost 2.5 over the past three years, is supporting consumer spending. 
AI FOMO (fear of missing out) is buoying enterprise spending on cloud services 
and the groundwork for AI initiatives. Those are beginning to bear fruit in terms of 
personal productivity, cost savings and more targeted products and services. 
4.3 Investor watchpoints 
The arrival of ASI would be a disruptive economic shock. Investors need to be alert 
for the early signals. 
Rather than relying on a single probability forecast, investors should watch five 
indicators particularly closely for signs that AGI is nearing.  
1. AI speeds up AI 
• Track METR’s estimate of how much AI speeds up AI. Currently the 
capabilities of models are doubling roughly every three months. In a 
world of RSI, the pace of this speed-up will itself likely accelerate. 
2. AI can complete whole jobs 
• Track via the Remote Labor Index, a multi-sector benchmark. Currently it 
finds that the highest performing agent still only manages to automate 
20 percent of the real-world, economically valuable freelance projects it 
attempts. 
3. Compute remains scarce 
• On a path to superintelligence, demand for compute will continue to 
outrun supply. Track GPU one-year rental prices, up by about a third over 
the past year. 
4. Firms bifurcate 
• Leaders and laggards in the same industry diverge while averages stay 
the same. Look at revenue per employee in top - and bottom -quartile 
firms 
5. Junior hiring comes under significant pressure 
• … And then the pressure rises up the ranks.

## Document 346: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-05)

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 398: GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～.pdf

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026年 11月 9日

1/3 
GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし 
～2026年 9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. 2026年 7～9月の収益率はマイナス 1.65％ 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）は 2026年 11月6日、同年 7～9月の運用実績を公表
した。期間収益率はマイナス1.65％だった。7～9月は国内外の長期金利が上昇し、債券価格が下
落した。加えて円高も進み、外国資産の円換算額を押し下げた。資産別の収益率は国内債券がマ
イナス 1.80％、外国債券がマイナス6.52％、国内株式がプラス3.71％、外国株式がマイナス
1.86％となった。9月末の運用資産額は314兆3,876億円と6月末から約3.4兆円減少した。 
GPIFを巡っては、片山さつき財務・金融相が7月に「日本の金融資産にさらなる投資をしてい
ただくという方向で後押しをする方策を追求したい」と発言し、ポートフォリオに変化があるか
注目が集まっていた。長期金利が上昇していたこともあり、特に国内債への関心が高かった。 
結論から言うと、国内債比率は基本ポートフォリオの25％に極めて近く、運用姿勢の変化を読
み取ることは難しい。国内債の比率を引き上げるのではないかという観測は一旦後退するだろ
う。

2. 国内債券比率は 25％近辺を維持  
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは国内外の株式と債券をそれぞれ25％とし、国内債券には±6ポイ
ントの乖離許容幅が設定されている。GPIFの公表資料によると、年金積立金全体に占める国内債
比率は 2026年9月末時点で26.49％だった。これには年金特別会計で管理する積立金、約 5.4兆
円が含まれている。 
この積立金を国内債券と年金積立金全体から除き、GPIFの運用資産ベースで計算すると、9月末
の国内債券比率は25.23％となる。6月末の24.98％から 0.25ポイント上昇したが、基本ポートフ
ォリオの25％をわずかに上回る水準だ（資料1）。過去の実績と比較しても、今回の比率は突出し
ていない。2020～2025年度の 9月末平均は 25.05％で、2026年9月末はこれを0.18ポイント上回
る程度だ。

### Page 2

2/3 
資料 1 GPIFの運用資産構成比率（推計、2026年 6月末・9月末）

（注）年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いたGPIF運用資産ベース。 
（出所）GPIF「2026年度第 1四半期運用状況（速報）」、「同第 2四半期運用状況（速報）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成。

3. 国内債券の買越額は推計 1.4兆円  
次に、7～9月の国内債券への買越額を推計する。6月末と9月末の国内債券残高の変化から、
期間中の運用損益による増減を除いて推計した。 
年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いた国内債券残高は、6月末の79兆3,840億円から9月末
の79兆3,206億円へ、634億円減少した。一方、7～9月の国内債券の運用損益はマイナス1兆
4,452億円だった。この差額から、国内債券への買越額は約1.4兆円と推計される（注 1）。4～6
月の約 5.7兆円と比較すると、国内債券への資金配分額は減少した（注2）。 
同じ方法で他の資産への資金配分を試算すると、外国債券は約3.3兆円の買い越しとなった。
一方、国内株式は約2.0兆円、外国株式は約0.8兆円の売り越しとなった。

4. 国内債券の運用姿勢に変化はみられず  
2026年7～9月の運用実績をみる限り、GPIFが国内債券の乖離許容幅を積極的に活用し、基本ポ
ートフォリオの25％を上回る水準へと比率を引き上げようとする動きは確認できなかった。 
国内債券は買い越しているとみられるものの、外国債券には国内債券を上回る資金が配分され
ている。4資産の比率はいずれも25％近い。基本ポートフォリオに近づけて運用する従来の姿勢
が維持されていると考えられる。 
以 上

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

【注釈】
1) 買越額は国内債券残高の変化から運用損益を控除した推計値。国内債券には為替ヘッジ付き外
国債券や円建て短期資産が含まれるため、日本国債の純購入額とは必ずしも一致しない。
2) 2026年7～9月は、GPIFの運用資産額の増減から運用収益額を差し引くと、約1.9兆円の資金
流入があった計算になる。4～6月はほぼゼロだった。こうした資金流入の違いも、国内債券の
買越額を比較する際には考慮する必要がある。

## Document 401: Hotter US inflation strengthens the case for a December Fed hike

Source PDF: all report pdf/Hotter US inflation strengthens the case for a December Fed hike.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

US consumer price inﬂation came in hotter than
expected, with headline CPI rising 0.4% month-on-month and
core CPI up 0.3%, both a tenth of a percentage point above
expectations. No year-on-year rates were published because
the government shutdown meant no price data was collected
in October 2025
James Knightley
Used vehicle prices fell sharply in October
There was some softness in the details of today’s US CPI report,
with new vehicle prices rising just 0.2% month-on-month and used

vehicle prices falling 1.2%. But other components were hotter:
medical care commodities rose 0.5%, transportation services
jumped 0.8%, while shelter, the largest component of the CPI
basket, increased 0.3%. Overall, headline CPI rose 0.4% and core
0.3%, both 0.1ppt above consensus.
Following on from Fed Governor Chris Waller's comments earlier
today that he backs a 25bp hike in December and could even opt
for a 50bp hike, Treasury yields have pushed higher, with the 10Y

and 2Y up 6bp and 13bp, respectively. Markets are now fully pricing
in a 25bp hike at the December Fed meeting, in line with our own

Nonetheless, hopes of a Middle East de-escalation are pulling
energy costs lower, with retail gasoline prices now below $4/gallon
for the ﬁrst time since mid-July. If that continues, it should provide
a meaningful drag on November headline inﬂation, but we won't
get that data until the day after the December FOMC meeting.
The Fed may get a sneak peek of that data, giving it the conﬁdence
to only raise rates by 25bp. We are not forecasting any further hikes

in 2027 as interest rate-sensitive sectors cool further, with housing
costs and lower energy prices amid weak wage growth dampening
price pressures in the economy.

產業聚焦降息前的美國汽車產業投資布局
2026/10/30
• 美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款
週期
• 高油價使油電、電動車需求
提升
• 自駕車、軟體營收增加帶動
車商獲利
美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款週期，獲利表現有望提升
美國傳統車廠近年轉向生產更多高價卡車和SUV，因部分SUV和皮卡的調整後息稅前利潤率高達20%、超
過其他車型，也使美國新車平均交易價格在9月超過5萬美元。福特與通用汽車即將在2026年年底至2027
年迎來全尺寸皮卡改款與更新計畫，由於在推出新款或新一代卡車後，通常會有2~3年較佳的銷售期，預
估福特和通用汽車在2027年及之後的獲利有機會持續提升。
美國政策轉為支持燃油車發展，但高油價使油電、電動車需求提升

儘管美國政策轉向支持燃油車發展 ，但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下 ，使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔率
暴衝至16%，包括豐田 、現代等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升 。美國3Q26電動車銷量約佔新車總銷量
6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩腳步 。由於油價上升使全球對電動車需求
增加，特斯拉2Q26、3Q26交車量也優於市場預期。
自駕車接受度提升，軟體營收增加帶動車商獲利
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接受度正持續提升，而各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱服務布局。通用
汽車預估其OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營收在2026年將突破30億美元，福特汽車的軟體付費訂閱用
戶已達160萬戶。3Q26特斯拉FSD活躍用戶數已達174萬、年增67%，且北美新車FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於10%的硬體銷售利潤率，軟體毛利率高達50~70%，且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。

美國9月份輕型車年化銷售量約為
1,600萬輛，低於市場預估共識的
1,630萬輛 。儘管9月初的勞動節
促銷活動使購車回饋及零售銷售
增加，但同時銷售量年減 4%也顯
示市場需求正面臨更大挑戰。
今年上半年儘管面臨高利率 、高
車價等負面影響 ，但高收入族群
支撐使美國車市仍具韌性 。然而
隨著利率上升 、對消費者可負擔
性的懷疑日益增加 ，市場目前正
逐步將2026年美國輕型車銷售量
預估由 1,610萬輛下修至 1,560至
1,600萬輛區間。
聯準會在 9月升息一碼 ，使美國
目標利率區間上升至 3.75~4%，
且目前 市場普遍預期聯準會在今
年12月及2027年上半年將再各升
息一碼 。美國約有 75~85%新車
購買使用貸款或融資 ，利率持續
上升將直接推高車貸利率 ，進而
增加消費者的購車成本與負債壓
力，且利率上升也會造成核貸門
檻轉趨嚴格 ，使消費者對新車的

需求下降。
未來若油價 、通膨下降 ，市場重
新步入降息循環後有望使美國車
市需求重新回升。資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/9/24，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/22

車商將迎來皮卡改款週期
回饋優惠在交易價中佔比美國新車交易價格美國新車平均交易價格( ATP )在
9月超過5萬美元，車價上漲在每

年下半年為常見現象 ，以因應車
款更新而重新定價 。然而 ，9月
ATP年增率低於1%遠低於典型的
9月增幅2~4%，顯示利率上升後
平價車款銷售相對強勁。
儘管美國新車售價成長幅度趨緩 ，
但其上升趨勢仍使美國傳統車商
利潤率逐步改善 。通用、福特汽
車等傳統車廠近年 轉向生產更多
卡車和 SUV，因部分 SUV和皮卡
的調整後息稅前利潤率超過 20%，
使得車廠利潤率能維持一定水準 。
(美元)

預估福特和通用汽車在 2027年及

4
高油價使美國電動車銷售逐步回溫
資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28
需求使油電、電動車並存
康寧儘管政策轉向支持燃油車發展 ，
但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下 ，
使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔
率暴衝至 16%，包括豐田 、現代
等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升 。

而儘管美國3Q26電動車銷量較去
年同期下降 (3Q25為電動車補助
期限 ，基期較高 )，但仍較 2Q26
成長近8%，約佔美國新車總銷量
的6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯
示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩
腳步。以市場反應來看 ，美國車
市正從先前單一電動化路線 ，轉
向燃油 、油電、電動車並存局面 。
美國國會在 2025年9/30 正式取
消購買或租賃新電動車的 7,500
美元稅收抵免 ，造成美國電動車
銷售在 4Q25開始大幅下滑 。另
外，川普政府在今年 9月底正式
宣布放寬美國企業燃油效率標準 ，
將未來新車的燃油效率年均提升
目標下調 ，使部分車商開始降低
電動車生產以減少虧損。
傳統車企如福特 、通用汽車的電
動車部門 EBIT利潤率可能介於 -
10~-30%以下，燃油效率標準放
寬使其生產方向轉回燃油車發展 ，
也有助於改善整體獲利結構。

通用汽車遞延收入上升，顯示未來軟體營收增加
資料來源：公司，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/29
軟體營收增加帶動獲利表現
各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱
服務布局，並將其視為未來重要獲
利成長動能 。 通 用 汽 車 預 估 其
OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營

收在 2026年將突破 30億美元 ，並
在未來維持雙位數成長 。福特汽車
的軟體付費訂閱用戶已達 160萬戶。
3Q26特斯拉 FSD活躍用戶數已達
174 萬 、 年增 67% ， 且 北 美 新 車
FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於 10%的硬體銷售
利潤率，軟體毛利率高達 50~70%，
且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接
受度正持續提升 。過去十年來 ，對
自駕車有較多了解的民眾比例由
40%增至58%，且近半數消費者相
信在有生之年將能看到自駕車普及 。
另外，消費者雖然支持車輛自動化
發展，但仍傾向保有人工接管能力 ，
因此對 Level 4 的 興 趣 最 高， 對
Level 5的興趣反而下降。
調查也發現，多數曾搭乘Robotaxi
的使用者對自動駕駛的觀感明顯改
善，未來隨著服務覆蓋範圍持續擴
大，Robotaxi有望進一步提升大眾
對自駕技術的信心與接受度。 資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/9/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/29

將普及比例

## Document 627: 凱基投顧_2356 英業達_向子慧_20261110

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2356 英業達_向子慧_20261110.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 10, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  電子 ‧ 台灣

英業達 
(2356.TW /2356 TT)

2027年AI伺服器系統出貨量可望攀升 
重要訊息 
英業達公告 3Q26 每股盈餘 0.71 元，落後凱基和市場預估，主要因獲利率
不如預期。管理層預估 2027 年伺服器、筆電與智慧裝置營收、出貨量都將
較 2026 年成長。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 財報不如預期。 3Q26 EPS 為 0.71 元，季減 35%、年減 7%，低於凱
基預估及市場共識。毛利率 4.2%、營業利益率 1.6%，均弱於凱基預期。
儘管 3Q26 伺服器營收占比由  2Q26 的 48% 升至 51%，毛利率仍季持平，
因高 ASP 的 AI 伺服器機櫃 (B300 與 GB300) 貢獻增加而稀釋毛利率，同時
出貨量成長有限。 此外，零組件成本上升使  NB ASP 提高，同樣拖累毛利
率，加上出貨量季減 9% (至 490 萬台)。同時營業費用上揚，因而營利率季
減且年減。 加上利息支出增加， 3Q26 業外虧損高於預期 ，因此  3Q26 EPS 
不如預期。1Q -3Q26 EPS 為 2.47 元，年增 34%。 
4Q26 進入產品過渡期與傳統淡季； 然 2027 年展望正向。受零組件供給受
限影響 (記憶體、CPU 與伺服器 PCB)，預期英業達 4Q26 出貨與營收受限，
凱基預估營收季減。 NB 出貨預期季減雙位數， 而伺服器營收將有更多貢獻
來自 ASP 較低的 ASIC 與通用型伺服器，因此伺服器營收可能季減， 但有利
毛利率。凱基預估  4Q26 營收將季減  14%，弱於先前預估。為反映  2H26 
營收與利潤率較預期弱，將 2026 年 EPS 預估下修至 2.99 元，年增 23%。
展望 2027 年，管理層表示伺服器將是主要成長動能， 營收年增雙位數，NB 
與智慧裝置出貨及營收則年增個位數。伺服器營收將因 明年取得多項 AI 伺
服器專案而成長，涵蓋 ASIC (自建) 與 GPU (Nvidia (美 )、AMD (美)) 平台。
不過，通用型伺服器出貨成長 來自 CSP 將 強於企業端，因企業端將 有預算
排擠。預期公司 2027 年 L10/L11 AI 伺服器出貨增加，AI 伺服器營收明年
預估年增逾 50%，占伺服器營收逾 70%。據此預估 伺服器營收占比將由  
2026 年的約 47% 擴大至 2027 年的 59-60%。然而，毛利率將承受壓力，
但毛利額仍將增加有利獲利成長， 為反映較低的營收預期，將  2027 年 EPS 
預估下修至 4.16 元 (年增 39%)，並預期 1Q27 營收季增。公司中長期營業
利益率目標為  2%，可能於 2H27- 2028 年達成。  
投資建議  
有鑑於目前評價與同業相比不具吸引力，凱基維持英業達「持有」評等 ，目
標價則由 65 元下修至 54 元，相當於 2027 年下修後每股盈餘的 13 倍。 
投資風險  
零組件供應緊俏；通膨影響終端需求、台幣升值 。

彈。 2027 年展望正向，在取得多項 AI
專案的情況下，伺服器業務將成為明年
營運成長主力。

圖 4：公司概況  圖 5：筆電、伺服器業務為主要營收來源 
成立於 1975 年，英業達在台灣高科技業佔有舉足輕重的地
位。英業達原以生產電子計算機起家，現已邁入高科技電子產
品的設計與製造，包括筆電、商用伺服器。英業達也併入英華
達，以整合旗下智慧型裝置產品，另 投資太陽能事業，以擴展
業務範疇。 2025 年營收約 51%來自筆電，3 %來自智慧型裝
置，46%來自伺服器， 2024 年佔比則分別為 50%、4%與
46%。

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合
物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

## Document 652: 凱基投顧_6223 旺矽_許芝瑄_20261111

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_6223 旺矽_許芝瑄_20261111.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 11, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC封裝/測試 ‧ 台灣

旺矽 
(6223.TWO /6223 TT )

營收及獲利持續創高，測試介面族群首選  
重要訊息 
旺矽公布 3Q26 財報，營收與獲利持續創高 。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 毛利率優於預期， 4Q26 營收與獲利持續創高。旺矽 3Q26 營收季增
12.8%，年增 72.9%，低於凱基預期，但超越公司原先展望。我們認為預估
落差主要來自營收認列時點差異，並非需求放緩。 毛利率季增 3.3ppts 至
61.7%，優於預期，反映 產品組合進一步轉佳。獲利季增 18.2%，EPS 為
18.38 元。展望 4Q26，ASIC 探針卡持續出貨， 管理層預期 營收季增 5-
10%，毛利率有望維持 3Q26 水準，延續營收與獲利持續創高的趨勢。  
ASIC 與 CPU 題材兼具，毛利率呈向上趨勢 ，為測試介面族群首選。 凱基預
估 2027 年 AI ASIC 出貨和成長性將首超 GPU。旺矽身為 ASIC 探針卡主要
供應商，ASIC 專案數為我們研究涵蓋的測試介面業者中最高，可望充分受
惠於 ASIC 需求擴張。同時，CPU 需求強勁，打入 CPU 探針卡供應鏈亦增添
成長動能。隨新增產能陸續開出，公司將持續承接客戶需求，支撐 2027-28
年營收成長達 75.6%及 35.5%。此外，MEMS 探針卡占比提升帶動產品組
合轉佳，毛利率上升趨勢明確，使獲利成長分別達 103.2 %及 44.8%，優於
營收。相較台灣同業毛利率較易隨季度產品組合波動，旺矽為我們研究涵蓋
的台灣測試介面業者中，唯一毛利率呈穩健上升趨勢的公司。綜上所述，我
們認為旺矽應享有相對同業的評價溢價，維持其為測試介面族群首選。  
CPO 設備貢獻時程 待釐清，惟評價進一步下修風險有限 。由於 Insertion  2
機台開發難度高，旺矽 CPO 設備的營收貢獻時點具不確定性。公司仍持續
與主要客戶合作開發，且客戶 並無跳過此測試階段的規劃。我們對 2027 年
CPO 設備營收貢獻維持較保守看法，預估占整體營收比重僅 中個位數。市場
對量產時程不確定性 及相關營收貢獻的疑慮，已對股價表現造成壓力，惟考
量市場對此業務的預期已偏悲觀，我們認為評價進一步下修風險有限。  
投資建議  
考量市場風險偏好下降，以及 CPO 設備的疑慮，我們將目標本益比調降至
35 倍，目標價調降至 7,000 元。此次調整主要反映評價假設轉趨審慎，我們
對核心業務需求及產能擴充的看法仍維持正向。維持「增加持股」投資評
等。 
投資風險  
競爭加劇；需求轉弱；全球經濟衰退。  
增加持股‧維持

圖 4：公司概況  圖 5：2025年產品組合

試、散熱解決方案。公司之探針卡解決方案包含懸臂式探針卡
(CPC)、垂直式探針卡 (VPC)及微機電式探針卡 (MEMS)。公司於
2024 年全球探針卡市場中排名第四。2025 年產品營收比重為探
針卡 72%、設備 26%與其他 2%。

## Document 665: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_1476 儒鴻_劉昃恩_20261110

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_1476 儒鴻_劉昃恩_20261110.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 10, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  1

法說重點摘要

儒鴻 
(1476 TT, NT$348.5, 增加持股)

重點摘要  
1. 目前訂單能見度 保持 6 個月水準；預計 4Q26 旺季營收季增 。明年
營收及毛利率展望初步審慎樂觀看待。  
2. 美國期中選舉後，短期尚未看到明顯追加訂單；但中期而言，隨著
關稅壁 壘政策趨穩與通膨控制政策可能推出，不確定性因素下降。
管理層預 期，品牌商最快 1Q27 才會作出訂單因應變化。  
3. 當前產能 利用率 達滿載。印尼 三期擴廠如計畫進行 。預計 2026 年
資本支出 6-8 億美元， 2027 年進一步增加至 10-12 美元。 
結論 
憑藉著新客戶加入與新產品採用增加，儒鴻營收及獲利成長動能有望
延伸至 2027 年。目前我們給予 「增加持股 」評等及目標價 447 元。 
3Q26 財報 
 3Q26 稅後淨利 17.4 億元，或 每股盈餘 6.36 元，年增 6%、季增
12%，符合預期 。 
 3Q26 營收年增 10%、季增 5%至 105.7 億元， 動能來自 成衣銷售
年增 16%，抵銷布料 1%衰退。 
 受惠新產品與新客戶 帶動 平均銷售單價 提升 ，及匯率有利， 3Q26
毛利率 年增 2.5、季增 1.2 個百分點至 31.2% ；然而， 員工季獎金
增加推升費用率 ，營利率 20.7% ，年增收斂至 1.6 個百分點 。

營運展望更新 
 目前訂單能見度 保持 6 個月水準 ；預計 4Q26 旺季營收季增。  
 2027 年營收及毛利率 展望初步審慎樂觀看待 ，細節將於 11 月底至
12 月初經內部確認後提供 。 
 美國期中選舉後， 短期尚未看到明顯追加訂單；但中期而言， 隨著
關稅壁壘政策趨穩 與通膨控 制政策可能推出 ，不確定性因素 下降。
管理層預 期，品牌商最快 1Q27 才會作出訂單因應變化。 
 新客戶 銷售佔比近月來從 10% 提升至 約 11%，反映消費者 對創新
產品需求 熱絡。此外， 當前仍有數個潛在新客戶洽談中， 部分案件
成功機率較高。  
 前幾大客戶營收佔比無重大變化。 品牌商仍著重於新品開發，走在
市場前端；通路商則尚在觀望期中選舉後 消費者動向。 
 公司採購策略傾向與長期供應商配合，以降低原料成本波動影響。  
 當前產能利用率 達滿載。印尼 三期擴廠如計畫進行 。

### Page 2

台灣 儒鴻

十一月 10, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

 印尼產品組合與越南差距已較過往縮小；但整體而言，越南對複雜
產品線掌握度還是較高，工人技術較成熟。  
 印尼物流時間較長，不確定性因素也較多。  
 預計 2026 年資本支出 6-8 億美元 (對比 1Q-3Q26 約 4-5 億美元)，
並於 2027 年進一步增加至 10-12 美元，主要用於印尼新廠擴建 ，
其次為 越南及柬埔寨廠產線調整與自動化導入 。 
 目前協力廠商供貨佔比 27-28%。 
 成衣產業庫存在過去一段時間緩慢減少，而零售價格呈走升趨勢。  
 隨著美國關稅退款給品牌商 ，品牌也可能討論並制定進一步退還給
供應商之方案。  
 股利政策 將延續過往水準  (過去五年配息率約 65-75%) 。

## Document 725: 大摩警告：旧存储价格正在见顶，2028年供需或转向宽松

Source PDF: all report pdf/大摩警告：旧存储价格正在见顶，2028年供需或转向宽松.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

摩根⼠丹利指出，美国中选后，预计 2028 年美国数据中⼼约 20% 的新增容量将推迟⾄ 2029
年落地，这可能在 2028 年为供应链制造 " 断层 " ，引发旧存储库存积压⻛险。旧存储受固定
价格⻓期协议约束较少，供需缺⼝收窄将⾯临较⼤的价格波动。上述分析仅针对旧存储，
包括 DDR3/4 、 SLC/MLC NAND 及 NOR 闪存。
摘要：
旧存储芯⽚市场的定价红利正逐步⾛向终结。摩根⼠丹利在最新报告中警告，云端资本⽀出不
确定性上升、消费端需求持续疲软，以及供需关系料将于 2028 年前后趋于平衡，旧存储⾏业的
盈利逻辑已从 " 向上弹性 " 转向 " 向下防御 " 。
据追⻛交易台，摩根⼠丹利发布报告指出，美国中期选举后，预计 2028 年美国数据中⼼约 20%
的新增容量将推迟⾄ 2029 年落地，涉及约 5 吉⽡的建设规模，相当于全球 2028 年新增容量的
10% 。
这⼀延迟可能在 2028 年为科技供应链制造 " 断层 " ，压缩旧存储芯⽚的定价空间，并引发库存积

压⻛险。
与此同时，摩根⼠丹利对 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 、 NOR 及 DDR4 四⼤产品的供需模型进⾏更
新后发现，各细分市场均呈现趋于平衡的态势，尤其以 2028 年为甚。
报告对市场的直接影响已经显现：摩根⼠丹利下调了多只旧存储相关个股的评级，并⼤幅削减
⽬标价，同时下调 2027 ⾄ 2028 年盈利预期。分析师将 1.0 倍市净率（ P/B ）作为估值下⾏锚，⽽
⾮以盈利⾼峰作为⽀撑底部，标志着市场对该板块的定价逻辑出现系统性转变。
定价优势成 " 双刃剑 " ，需求⾛弱暴露脆弱性
摩根⼠丹利此前的建仓逻辑建⽴在旧存储芯⽚的差异化定价能⼒上 —— 相较于主流存储，旧存
储受固定价格⻓期协议约束较少，因此在短缺周期中能更快速地提价。然⽽，该⾏现在认为，
这种灵活性正在演变为双向⻛险。
报告指出，云端⽀出可⻅度正在下滑，企业端增量需求的确定性随之减弱；与此同时，消费端
需求⼤幅⾛软，终端客⼾承接进⼀步涨价的能⼒受到明显制约。
摩根⼠丹利的 PC 半导体研究显⽰， 2026 年⼀季度⾄三季度出现了集中采购的 " 拉货 " 现象，但四

季度随即进⼊更深度的库存消化阶段，表明此前的订单规模⾼于实际消费需求，存在透⽀之
嫌。
报告特别说明，上述分析仅针对旧存储领域，包括 DDR3/4 、 SLC/MLC NAND 及 NOR 闪存，不
涉及全球主流 DDR5 或 TLC/QLC NAND 市场。
供需模型更新：短缺窗⼝正在收窄
摩根⼠丹利对 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 、 NOR 及 DDR4 的最新供需模型均指向同⼀趋势：供需
缺⼝逐步收窄， 2028 年前后或实现基本平衡乃⾄出现过剩。
具体预测显⽰，2028 年 SLC NAND 、 MLC NAND 和 NOR 的供需缺⼝分别约为 -3% 、 -5%
和 -1% ，⽽ DDR4 则可能出现约 +1% 的供给过剩。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

分析师指出，近期短缺局⾯未必会⽴即消散，但缺⼝收窄本⾝⾜以削弱增量定价能⼒，并早于
盈利⾼点触发股价重估。
基于此，摩根⼠丹利下调了对多个产品线 2027 ⾄ 2028 年的 ASP 假设： NOR 及 DDR4 的 ASP 预测
在 2028 年四季度分别被削减约 23% ⾄ 26% ；部分公司的 SLC NAND ASP 预测降幅则更为显著。
在出货量预测基本不变的情况下，收⼊和⽑利率估算随之下移， 2028 年 EPS 预测普遍被下调
10% ⾄ 30% 以上。
数据中⼼资本⽀出延迟： 2028 年供应链 " ⽓⽳ " ⻛险
美国中期选举后出台的数据中⼼新⽴法是此轮预期转变的重要触发因素。
据摩根⼠丹利美国团队测算，德克萨斯州和俄亥俄州等地的⽴法变化导致约 5 吉⽡的数据中⼼建
设从 2028 年推迟⾄ 2029 年，这意味着美国 2028 年数据中⼼新增容量将同⽐下降约 4% ， 2029 年
则将⼤幅反弹约 38% 。
在全球层⾯，摩根⼠丹利现预计 2028 年全球数据中⼼新增容量同⽐增⻓约 9% ， 2029 年则升⾄约
20% 。
这⼀ " 前低后⾼ " 的节奏对旧存储⾏业构成阶段性压⼒ —— 需求延迟叠加供给持续爬坡，2028 年极
可能形成阶段性供⼤于求的局⾯，届时库存周期⻛险将显著上升。
值得关注的是，摩根⼠丹利维持对全球超⼤规模云⼚商资本⽀出的总量预测不变，预计 2027 年
约 1.6 万亿美元、 2028 年约 1.8 万亿美元，但明确指出若建设进度进⼀步推迟， 2028 年的预测存
在下⾏偏差⻛险。
估值框架转向：以账⾯价值为下⾏锚
⾯对盈利预期的系统性下修，摩根⼠丹利将估值⽅法论调整为以 1.0 倍市净率作为下⾏估值锚，
取代此前以盈利⾼峰作为估值底部的框架。
分析师认为，对于资本密集型 IDM 及晶圆代⼯企业⽽⾔，账⾯价值在周期性低⾕中具有⼼理⽀撑
意义 —— 当盈利暂时承压时，投资者通常会更多地回归净资产基础进⾏估值。
在各细分公司层⾯，⽬标价调整幅度相当显著，但 IDM 类企业与⽆晶圆⼚（ Fabless ）设计公司
之间的处理⽅式有所区别。
报告明确指出， 1.0 倍 P/B 的框架主要适⽤于 IDM/ 晶圆代⼯类企业，对 Fabless 公司并不适⽤同⼀
估值⽅法论，因为后者缺乏资产负债表层⾯的估值地板⽀撑，在周期下⾏中⾯临更⾼的波动⻛
险。
分析师将价格放缓、订单削减及库存消化列为主要下⾏催化剂；云端订单需求重新⾛强，或供
给扩张进度延迟，则是当前情境下最主要的上⾏⻛险。

## Document 739: 実質賃金は９ヵ月連続プラス、夏のボーナスも大幅増（26年9月毎月勤労統計） ～先行きは物価上昇が懸念材料。実質賃金マイナス転化の可能性も～

Source PDF: all report pdf/実質賃金は９ヵ月連続プラス、夏のボーナスも大幅増（26年9月毎月勤労統計） ～先行きは物価上昇が懸念材料。実質賃金マイナス転化の可能性も～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/5 
実質賃金は９ヵ月連続プラス、夏のボーナスも大幅増（26 年 9 月毎月勤労統計） 
～先行きは物価上昇が懸念材料。実質賃金マイナス転化の可能性も～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年９月の毎月勤労統計では、現金給与総額が前年比＋4.6％、実質賃金が同＋2.2％と、
ともに高い伸びとなった。 共通事業所ベースでも現金給与総額は同＋4.4％、 実質賃金も同＋
2.0％程度とプラスを維持。所得環境の改善が続いており、良好な結果と評価できる。 
○ 現金給与総額の伸びの拡大には振れの大きい特別給与の急増の影響が大きく、割り引いてみ
る必要がある。もっとも、こうした攪乱を受けない共通事業所ベースの所定内給与も前年比
＋3.3％と高い伸びを維持しており、賃金の基調は引き続き良好である。 
○ 2026 年夏のボーナスは前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加。 全事業所ベースでも同＋5.9％と大
幅増となり、賞与の支給事業所割合も上昇した。企業収益の底堅さや人手不足を背景に、冬
のボーナスも増加する可能性が高い。 
○ 先行きも名目賃金は高い伸びを維持するとみられる一方、物価上昇率は年末にかけて高まる
見込み。電気・ガス代支援を織り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアが前年比＋３％台に達
するとみられ、実質賃金が再びマイナスに転じるリスクがあることには注意が必要。 
○ 2027 年春闘に向けた労使双方の賃上げ姿勢に、前年からの明確なトーンダウンはみられな
い。初動のモメンタムは良好と評価できる。

1. 名目賃金、実質賃金とも増加で良好な内容 
厚生労働省から公表された2026 年９月の毎月勤労統計は、名目賃金、実質賃金ともに高い伸び
となり、総じて良好な結果となった。所定内給与の高い伸びが続くなか、実質賃金もプラス圏を
維持しており、家計の所得環境は引き続き改善している。 
現金給与総額は前年比＋4.6％と、８月の同＋3.9％から伸びが拡大した。内訳では、所定内給
与が同＋3.7％、所定外給与が同＋3.6％と引き続き高い伸びだったことに加え、特別給与も同＋
32.6％と大幅に増加した。また、名目賃金から物価変動の影響を除いた実質賃金は前年比＋2.2％
と、８月の同＋1.6％から伸びが拡大した。これで９ヵ月連続のプラスであり、実質賃金の改善が
続いている。

### Page 2

2/5 
なお、報道等で言及されることの多いこの本系列は、調査対象事業所の部分入れ替えやベンチ
マーク更新等の影響を受けやすく、月次の賃金動向の基調を判断するうえでは必ずしも適さな
い。そうした観点からは、１年前と当月の双方で回答している調査対象のみに限定して集計した
「共通事業所」ベースの前年比を併せて確認することが望ましい。 
その共通事業所ベースでみると、９月の現金給与総額は前年比＋4.4％と、８月の同＋2.8％か
ら伸びが大きく拡大している。内訳では、一般労働者が同＋4.4％、パートタイム労働者が同＋
4.8％となった。本系列の＋4.6％と大きな差はなく、共通事業所ベースでも高い賃金上昇率が確
認できる。 
なお、ボーナス支給月以外の特別給与は月ごとの振れが大きいため、この動きをもって賃金の
基調が一段と強まったと判断することは避けたい。現金給与総額の伸びの拡大については、こう
した一時的な要因を割り引いてみる必要がある点には注意が必要だ。もっとも、そうした点を考
慮しても、基調を示す所定内給与は前年比＋3.3％と高い伸びを維持しており、賃金の基調は引き
続き強いとみて構わない。 
共通事業所ベースの現金給与総額を「持家の帰属家賃を除く総合」で実質化すると、９月の実
質賃金は前年比＋2.0％程度となる。本系列の同＋2.2％とほぼ同程度の伸びであり、共通事業所
ベースでも実質賃金は明確なプラスとなっている。９月は賃金の実質化に用いられる物価上昇率
が前年比＋2.4％と、８月の同＋2.2％から高まったが、名目賃金の伸びがこれを上回った。 
このように、特別給与の増加による押し上げは割り引いてみる必要があるものの、賃金の基調
を示す所定内給与も高い伸びを維持しており、内容も良好である。本系列、共通事業所のいずれ
でみても名目賃金が物価上昇を上回っており、所得環境の改善を改めて確認する内容だったと評
価できる。

（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」

### Page 3

3/5 
2. 所定内給与は＋３％台の高い伸びを維持 
所定内給与（共通事業所ベース、以下同じ）は前年比＋3.3％と、８月の同＋3.1％から伸びが
拡大した。これで４ヵ月連続の＋３％台となる。内訳では、一般労働者が同＋3.1％、パートタイ
ム労働者が同＋4.7％となった。一般労働者の所定内給与は８月の同＋2.9％から伸びを高め、再
び３％台となった。パートタイム労働者も＋５％近い高い伸びを維持しており、幅広い層で賃金
増加が続いている。パートについては、昨秋の最低賃金引き上げの影響に加え、深刻な人手不足
を背景として、企業が人材確保のため時給を引き上げていることが影響しているとみられる。 
所定内給与増加の背景にあるのが、2026 年春闘で実現した高い賃上げである。連合の春闘最終
集計では、賃上げ率が5.01％となった。2025 年春闘からはやや鈍化したものの、それでも５％台
の高い賃上げが実現した。また、組合員数300 人未満の組合でも4.69％と、前年の4.65％を小幅
に上回っており、中小企業でも高い賃上げが実施されている。 
春闘賃上げの給与への反映は７～８月頃までで概ね出揃う。そうしたなか、９月の所定内給与
が前年比＋3.3％と、８月からさらに伸びを高めたことは評価できる。春闘で決まった高い賃上げ
が実際の給与に着実に反映され、賃金上昇率が高い水準で維持されていることを示している。 
このように、９月の所定内給与は、春闘賃上げの反映が概ね出揃った後も＋３％台の高い伸び
を維持している。賃金の基調は引き続き良好と判断できる。人手不足を背景とした賃上げ圧力も
根強く、所定内給与は先行きも前年比＋２％台後半から＋３％強程度の高い伸びを維持する可能
性が高い。

3. 底堅い企業業績を背景に、夏のボーナスは明確に増加 
今回の毎月勤労統計では、特別集計として26 年夏季賞与の支給状況が公表されている。これに
